# Vossie Accounts (Sample Mode) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build stage D, the start of the signed-in half, as the spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-accounts-design.md` describes: a sample store that stands in for Supabase, sign-in by email code on one page that works without the app's JavaScript, sessions, the first-sign-in step, the account page, privacy settings with the data export and account deletion, and the shell's signed-in state. Tested at five screen sizes and within the JS budget.

**Architecture:**
- **A sample store on `globalThis`.** `lib/data/store.ts` keeps one `SampleStore` per server process (a `Map` per table), seeded from `data/fixtures/accounts.ts`. Only `lib/data/*.ts` touches it, through async functions shaped like the Supabase calls that replace them. Later stages add tables without a rewrite (see "Store design").
- **Plain forms, Server Actions, redirects.** Every form posts to a Server Action in `lib/auth/actions.ts`, which checks its input on the server, keeps errors in the store (never in the URL) and ends with `redirect()`. Without the app's JavaScript that's a 303 and a full page load; with it, Next renders the destination from the root and, because the action changed cookies, drops its kept copies of earlier pages.
- **The server is the only judge of the session.** `getViewer()` (`lib/auth/session.ts`) reads the `httpOnly` `vossie_session` cookie and looks its hash up in the store. The header and menu get the signed-in state as Server Component slots from the root layout, so no readable cookie and no client code are needed (plan decision 1).
- **No new client code on the sign-in and account pages.** The one new client component, `FlashToast`, is on the homepage only, for the "signed out" and "account deleted" toasts.

**Tech Stack:** As parts B and C left it:
- Next.js 16.4.0 with Cache Components and Partial Prefetching, React 19.3, TypeScript 5;
- Tailwind CSS 4.3 through PostCSS;
- pnpm 10.30;
- Vitest 5 with Testing Library and jsdom 30;
- Playwright 1.64 with axe-core.

**Sources:**
- **Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-accounts-design.md`. "Spec 2.5" in this plan means that spec's section 2, decision 5; "spec 3.1" means its section 3.1.
- **The specs that build on this one** (their tables must fit the store): `2026-10-14-messaging-design.md` (stage E), `2026-10-14-selling-design.md` (stage F) and `2026-10-14-staff-tools-design.md` (stage G).
- **Earlier specs** (tokens, breakpoints, motion, accessibility, budgets; the sign-in links): `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-homepage-design.md` and `2026-10-13-browse-and-listing-design.md` (section 5.9).
- **Decisions:** `docs/reference/LANDING-DECISIONS.md`, rows 54 to 56 for this stage's context.
- **Previous plans, for conventions and pitfalls:** `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-13-shopping-pages.md` ("Changes made during the build", especially `RefreshWhenStale` and the prefetch findings) and `2026-10-14-info-and-system-pages.md`.

## Before you start

**This plan starts after parts B and C.** When it was written (2026-10-14), part C was at its review (`60b466e`; its Task 10, the docs, still to come) and part B had a plan and no code. Start only once both have their final docs commit. Then work through "Recheck before starting" below before Task 1.

**Approvals Lama gives in chat before execution starts:**
1. **Git.** Implementers leave their work uncommitted. The controller checks each task and commits it (Lama's standing choice). `vossie-audit/` and `.superpowers/` stay out of git and untouched. `.claude/launch.json` has a change made outside this work: never stage it.
2. **Execution.** Subagent-driven, with one combined reviewer per build step (plan check first, then code quality), time-boxed. This is Lama's standing preference.
3. **Files this plan removes:** one. Task 4 removes `data/fixtures/viewer.ts` (the sample activity behind the old `VOSSIE_SAMPLE_SIGNED_IN` switch, which spec 2.9 retires). The implementer checks it with `ls` first, in its own tool call, then runs `rm data/fixtures/viewer.ts` and nothing else. No other file is removed or moved.
4. **New environment variables, both optional:** `VOSSIE_SAMPLE_MODE` (sample mode is on unless it's `0`) and `VOSSIE_EMAIL_DOMAINS` (defaults to `eduvos.com`). Task 15 documents them in `.env.example`.
5. **Sample wording.** The sample accounts' first names, and the sign-in and account pages' words where the spec gives none, are for Lama to change. Plan decisions 7 and 15 list them.

No installs are needed.

**Global safety rules (from `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, highest priority):**
- Check before acting (`ls`, `git status`), as a separate tool call from the action.
- Run git commands one at a time. Never run destructive git commands, never amend, rebase or reset, never touch `.git/` internals, and never `rm` anything except the one file in approval 3.
- Never kill an install or build that is still running.
- Port 3100 is Lama's dev server. Don't touch it. The e2e tests use 3200; the "looking at it" checks use 3201.
- Before you edit a shared file (`app/globals.css`, `app/layout.tsx`, `app/page.tsx`, `tests/e2e/*.spec.ts`, `docs/*`), read its current text and edit by the exact lines this plan quotes. Never paste back a whole list or file from an older copy.

## Plan decisions (made while planning)

Lama can veto any of these at plan review. In this plan, "plan decision N" means this list; "spec 2.N" means the spec's decisions; "decision N" on its own means a row in the decisions log.

1. **The header and menu learn the session from the server, and there's no readable `vossie_signed_in` cookie** (spec 2.5 and 3.4 change). The root layout passes each a slot: `<Suspense fallback={signed-out link}><HeaderAccount /></Suspense>`, a Server Component that calls `getViewer()`. `SiteHeader` and `MenuDrawer` just render the slot. This costs about nothing on every page (`UserIcon` even leaves the client bundle), where a cookie hook would cost bytes on every page and the listing page has about 95 B (part C measured a new client component in the layout at +472 B). With no readable cookie there is nothing for the server to distrust, and one cookie fewer (POPIA). It's the pattern messaging decision 9 already plans for the unread count. Every page gains this small per-request part, so pages that were wholly static become partly prerendered; their static shells don't change. Trade-off: a tab left open while you sign out in another tab keeps its old header until its next page load or form post.
2. **No stale pages after signing in or out.** Sign-in, sign-out and deletion are Server Actions that write cookies and end with `redirect()`. Without the app's JavaScript that's a 303 and a full page load. With it, Next renders the destination from the root (so the header slot changes too) and, because cookies changed, drops its kept copies of earlier pages and its prefetches, so Back asks the server again. Task 13 checks both paths, with Back, by link and with the bundle blocked.
3. **Errors live in the store, never in the URL.** A sign-in in progress is a row keyed by the hash of the `vossie_signin` cookie (`httpOnly`, 30 minutes); its errors, the typed email and the typed first name sit on that row. Signed-in forms keep their errors and typed values in a form-state table keyed by the session and the form, for 10 minutes. Stages E to G reuse it for their forms.
4. **Tokens are stored hashed, like codes.** The session and sign-in cookies hold a random UUID; the store keeps only its SHA-256. A leaked store can't sign anyone in, and the export has no ids to leave out.
5. **Sample mode's "email" is an outbox table.** The attempt keeps only the code's hash (spec 5). The plain code goes into `sampleEmails`, which stands in for the email service; the sign-in page reads it to show "Sample mode: your code is …" (spec 2.3). The real email service replaces that one table and one function.
6. **Codes:** hashed with the attempt's key as salt and compared with `timingSafeEqual`. Spaces are ignored. A code that isn't 6 digits says "Enter the 6-digit code." and doesn't use a try. Step 2 has **Send a new code** (spec 3.1 says "That code has expired. Send a new one.", so there must be a way to): a new code gives 5 fresh tries and counts towards the 5 an hour.
7. **Wording the spec doesn't give** (Lama may change it):
   - the rate limit: "You've asked for 5 codes in the last hour. Try again later.";
   - the field labels: "Your Eduvos email address", "6-digit code", "Your first name", "Your campus";
   - the error summary's heading: "There's a problem";
   - after a new code: "We sent a new code.";
   - the deletion box's error: "Tick the box to confirm you understand.";
   - with sample mode off: "Sign-in isn't available yet. You can still browse every campus and save listings on this device.";
   - the toasts: "You're signed out" and "Your account has been deleted";
   - after editing details: "Your details are saved.";
   - the hints: "We'll send you a 6-digit code to sign in.", "Sellers see it when you message them. 2 to 40 characters." and, over the sample accounts, "Sample mode only: straight in, no code.";
   - step 3's line: "First time on Vossie? Tell us two things and you're in.";
   - the privacy settings page's sentences about the download and deletion (Task 11).
8. **The account is made at step 3, not at the code.** A new email's right code moves the attempt to step 3; only **Continue** creates the account, records consent (at the time the box was ticked) and starts the session. Abandoning step 3 stores nothing. Existing accounts get a new consent row only when the policy version changes.
9. **Where a finished sign-in goes:** `next` when it's safe and isn't `/login` itself; otherwise `/account`.
10. **A signed-in visitor on `/login` sees "You're signed in as {email}"** with **Your account** and the back link (spec 3.1), rather than being bounced straight back (spec 2.11's "land back where they were"): a bounce would look as if Message seller did nothing. The back link takes them back.
11. **The error summary** has `role="alert"`, `tabIndex={-1}` and `autoFocus` (spec 6). On a page load, which is what a post without the app's JavaScript gives, the browser focuses it. With the JavaScript, React only autofocuses form controls, so the alert role announces it instead and focus stays on the button. Step 2's code field has `autoFocus` when there's no error, so arriving at step 2 puts you in the field either way.
12. **Toasts after signing out and deleting** (spec 2.13 asks for the second; the first matches it): the action sets `vossie_flash` (readable, 60 seconds, holds `signed-out` or `deleted`, never text). The homepage reads it inside `<Suspense>`, and a small client `FlashToast` shows the toast through the existing toast region and deletes the cookie, so it shows once. Only the homepage carries it. Without the app's JavaScript there's no toast; the header's "Sign in" shows the change.
13. **"Your details are saved." is a server-rendered status line,** not a toast, so the account pages keep no client code (spec 7). It's form state, so it shows until the next post or for 10 minutes.
14. **The menu's Sign out closes the menu first.** `closeOnLink` now also closes on a submit button, so the toast isn't hidden behind a modal (the toast region's rule). Signed in, the menu has 8 rows; from the tablet step the type then divides the spare height by 8 (a `:has()` rule), so signed-out visitors see exactly what they did.
15. **The sample accounts** are Thandi (student, Midrand), Sipho (approved seller, Tech corner, Midrand), Lerato (seller waiting for approval, Pretoria), Naledi (mentor, Midrand) and Pieter (admin, Pretoria). Their buttons say "Student, Midrand", "Seller, Tech corner", "Seller waiting for approval", "Mentor" and "Admin". Deleting a sample account works like any other; its button puts it back from the fixture, so testers can always use it.
16. **The account page's Selling row:** no shop: "Start selling" (`/sell`); pending: "Your shop", "Waiting for the Hub's approval" (`/sell`); approved: "Your shop" (the storefront); paused: the same, with "Paused" under it. Stage F points them at its own pages. Staff roles show as a line, "Hub role: Mentor" or "Hub role: Admin".
17. **`VOSSIE_SAMPLE_MODE=0`** makes `getViewer()` return null, every sign-in action refuse, and `/login` say sign-in isn't available yet (spec 2.1).
18. **Cookies are `Secure` when the request came over HTTPS,** read from `x-forwarded-proto` (Next sets it; hosts set `https`). Plain `next start` on localhost is HTTP, so tests see no `Secure`.
19. **The intermediate redirects use `replace`.** Every sign-in step comes back to the same `/login?next=…` with `RedirectType.replace`, so Back leaves sign-in in one step. The final redirect, sign-out and deletion push as usual.
20. **The export** (`/account/privacy/export`, spec 3.3) is `{ exportedAt, account, consents, sessions }` as `vossie-data.json`, `Cache-Control: no-store`. Sessions give only when they started and end. Later stages add keys (`messages`, `reports`, `listings`).
21. **Words that sign-in makes untrue change** (Task 12): the Saved note and the homepage's save note (saves stay on the device, spec 2.12, but "when sign-in arrives" is now wrong), two How it works answers, and the privacy policy, which gains the three new cookies by name. The privacy test checks them against the real constants, as it does the others.
22. **End to end:** each test that signs up uses its own new address; sample accounts are only ever signed in to, never edited or deleted, because every test shares one server and one store.

## Store design

How stages E to G add tables without a rewrite:

- **One object, many tables.** `lib/data/store.ts` keeps one `SampleStore` on `globalThis` (under `__vossieSampleStore`, with a `version`), so every route, Server Action and route handler in the process shares it, and `next dev` keeps it across reloads until `STORE_VERSION` changes. Each table is a `Map` keyed by its primary key.
- **Rows look like the Supabase tables.** Row types live in `lib/data/rows.ts`: ids are text, times are ISO 8601 strings in UTC, rows point at each other by id, and nothing holds a class or a function. Only `lib/data/*.ts` touches the store, through async functions that take `now` from their caller (so they never read the clock) and return copies.
- **Adding a table** is five small edits: its row type in `rows.ts`, a field in `SampleStore`, its seed in `seedStore()` (from a fixture that only uses `import type`), a bump of `STORE_VERSION`, and a domain module (`lib/data/messages.ts` and so on). If its rows hold personal data, add a line to `deleteAccountData` and `exportAccountData` in `lib/data/accounts.ts`.
- **What fits:** stage E's `conversations`, `messages`, `reports` and `notifications` (keyed by id, pointing at accounts and listings); stage F's `sellers`, `listings`, `photos` and `onboardingDrafts` (seeded from the existing fixtures; its cached readers wrap the store and are tagged, as its spec says); stage G's `auditLog`, `featuredDraws`, `featuredPins`, `pickupPoints` and `mentorNotes`. `sellerProfiles` (account to shop, with a status) is already here for F and G to extend, and the `formStates` table serves every signed-in form.

## The JS ledger

- **Limit:** 153,600 B (brotli, quality 5) on a first visit at 390px, for each page.
- **Stop rule (spec 7):** if any page passes **the stop line Lama last accepted (152,305 B when this plan was written; use the newer figure from "Recheck" item 2 if there is one)**, stop after that task and report. Don't cut features to fit; that's Lama's decision.
- **Where bytes move:**
  - Tasks 1 to 7 add no client code. The sign-in page's forms are Server Components posting to Server Actions, which the framework's own chunk already handles.
  - **Task 8 changes `SiteHeader` and `MenuDrawer`, which are in every page's shell.** It takes the account link out of the header (and `UserIcon` with it) and adds a slot prop to each; the menu's link check gains a selector. Expect every page to move by a few bytes, up or down. Task 8 measures right after.
  - Task 9 adds `FlashToast` (a client component) to the homepage only.
  - Tasks 10 and 11 add no client code.
- **Recording.** `tests/e2e/performance.spec.ts` prints each page's `scriptBrotli`. Tasks 7, 8, 9 and 15 run it at 390px and record the figures in their commit messages. Task 7 adds the sign-in page to it; Task 14 adds the account page, measured signed in.

| Task 8, the header and menu (`40f6451`) | 150,515 | 151,262 | 151,671 | 150,515 | n/a |
| Task 9, the homepage toast (`6e4bb91`) | 150,596 | 151,343 | 151,752 | 150,596 | n/a |
| **Final (Task 15)**, after the review fixes (`17dd972`) | **150,614** | **151,361** | **151,770** | **150,614** | **150,614** (signed in) |

The commits recorded the listing page's figure (and, for Task 7, the others); Task 8's and Task 9's other cells follow from their messages (every page 39 B lighter, then 81 B heavier). The review fixes added 18 B to every page (`FlashToast`'s early return), so the stage ends 60 B above where it started on every page that shares the shell. The account page has no client code of its own, so it matches the sign-in page to the byte (Task 14 measured it first, at 150,614 B). The highest page is the listing page, at 151,770 B: 535 B under the stop line and 1,830 B under the limit. The stop rule never fired.

The stop line used: 152,305 B (decision 52). Unit tests at the start: 753 (127 files); at the end 978 (153 files). End to end at the start: 646 pass, 464 skipped; at the end 734 pass, 541 skipped, none fail.

## Changes made during the build (2026-10-14)

Where the build differed from the tasks as written, with the commit that made each change. Tasks 1 (`544524e`), 3 (`48e9130`) and 12 (`e225993`) record no differences of their own; the later reviews below changed some of Task 3's files.

**Tasks 2 to 6**
- **`secrets.test.ts` checks behaviour instead of mocking `node:crypto`** (`7873527`, Task 2). Vitest runs this repo in jsdom, where a mock of a Node builtin reaches the test file but not the source module. The two things the mock proved are checked another way: 2,000 codes keep their leading zeros, and the source is read to confirm it calls `randomInt(` (never `Math.random`) and `timingSafeEqual(expected, actual)`.
- **`sastClock` reuses `lib/time.ts`'s existing `SAST_CLOCK`** instead of adding a second formatter (`7873527`).
- **`data/fixtures/viewer.ts` stays in place, unused** (`2f7c123`, Task 4, amendment 1). `getViewerContext` now asks the session and the `VOSSIE_SAMPLE_SIGNED_IN` switch is gone. The file is listed in `docs/PROGRESS.md` for Lama to decide on.
- **Tasks 5 and 6 are one commit** (`a1bef55`), because both build `lib/auth/actions.ts`.
- **The review of Tasks 1 to 6** (`1767777`):
  - `recordWrongTry` and `reserveCodeSend` (new, in `lib/data/sign-in.ts`) each count and change in one step. Overlap tests showed 3 parallel wrong posts counted once, and 4 parallel resends made 8 sends. `countCodesSent` and `recordCodeSent` stay as reads and setup for tests. Their comments say what the real backend must do atomically (go-live checklist).
  - Deleting an account also clears every outbox row sent to its address, found by address.
  - `createAccount` returns the existing account for a known email, so an email has one account ever and a second tab or double post doesn't overwrite its name or campus.
  - Emails must have an ASCII local part and a well-formed domain, so a look-alike letter can't pass as part of a listed domain. First names are composed as NFC, lose invisible and control characters, and need a letter. `maskEmail` keeps the first whole character.
  - Every email's old code-send records are swept, not only the one being used.
  - The config limits and `randomInt` are pinned by tests.

**Tasks 7 to 11**
- **The sign-in page** (`3f96aef`, Task 7) was built as written: no skeleton height needed changing, layout shift was 0 at 390, 768 and 1280, and the forms work with the bundle blocked.
- **The header and menu** (`40f6451`, Task 8) made every page 39 B lighter, as the plan expected (a few bytes, either way).
- **Every page paid 81 B for the homepage's toast** (`6e4bb91`, Task 9). `FlashToast` is on the homepage only, but the header's home link prefetches `/`, which pulls in the homepage's own chunk. Any client code used only on the homepage costs every page the same way. No page passed the stop line.
- **Tasks 10 and 11 are one commit** (`8d7e00f`), because `app/globals.css` and `routes.spec.ts` carry both. The plan's `.account-section a:not(.btn)` link rule would have turned the error summary's red links sky-dark, so it matches `.account-section p a:not(.btn)` (links in a paragraph only). No client code, so every page's JS is unchanged.

**The review of the whole stage** (`17dd972`)
- **`FlashToast` shows once.** Next keeps recent pages hidden in an `<Activity>` and shows them again on a link or Back, which re-runs the toast's effect, so the sign-out toast came back. The effect now returns early when the `vossie_flash` cookie is already gone. It costs 18 B on every page. New unit tests drive an `<Activity>` hidden and visible again.
- **Each error summary has its own id.** `ErrorSummary` takes an `id` (`signin-email-summary`, `signin-code-summary`, `signin-profile-summary`, `account-details-summary`, `account-privacy-delete-summary`) and its heading's id is `${id}-title`. The plan's fixed `form-summary-title` repeated across the pages Next keeps in the DOM. `components/forms/summary-ids.test.tsx` renders every form at once and checks no id repeats.
- **Sign-in errors show for 60 seconds after their post** (`SIGN_IN_ERRORS_SHOW_MS` in `lib/auth/config.ts`, and `postedAt` on the attempt). The attempt lasts 30 minutes, and a visit 20 minutes later opened on an old error summary that took focus. Typed values stay. This changes what a visitor sees, so it is listed in `docs/PROGRESS.md` and decision 66.

**Tasks 13 and 14** (`8b35535`, `039067e`)
- **`accounts.spec.ts` has one test more than the plan**, "the sign-out toast shows once": it signs out, leaves by the header's link and comes Back. Its check is a snapshot: a `MutationObserver` installed in the page counts toasts added after the Back, and `toastWatch` surviving proves the Back was a client-side return to the kept page and not a full load. An earlier draft used a retrying `toHaveCount(0)`, which passed even with the early return removed: a re-shown toast stays for 4 seconds and the assertion waits 5. With the early return disabled the test now fails at 390 and 1440.
- **Locators name the error summary** (`getByRole("alert", { name: "There's a problem" })`, or `.form-summary`) instead of a bare `getByRole("alert")`, because Next's route announcer is an alert too. Two tests also check that the header's account icon isn't a stale copy after Back.
- **`cls.spec.ts`:** `PAGES`'s type is wrapped over three lines (it is long). The layout shift on the new pages is far under the limit, so the skeleton's `min-height` didn't need to change.
- **No axe finding** needed a component or CSS change.

**Task 15**
- The review-screenshots test scrolls to the top before it opens the menu (`tests/e2e/screenshots.spec.ts`). After the privacy page's Delete button the header has folded to its crest, so the Menu button isn't there.
- CLAUDE.md already had a line saying every cookie and storage key is named `vossie_…` and the privacy page names each, so the plan's replacement for the "Collect the minimum." line leaves that sentence out rather than repeat it.
- The spec was already in line on decisions 1, 3, 4, 6, 10, 11 and 12 (amendment 2), so its edits here are the build's: the 60-second error window, the per-form summary ids, the one-step counters, one account per email, stricter emails and names, deletion's outbox rows, and the toast showing once.
- Decision 66 records the changes.

## Conventions for every task

- **Paths and commands.** Paths are relative to `/Users/LamaNkuna/Projects/Vossie`, and commands run from there.
- **Code style.** TypeScript, double quotes, semicolons, 2-space indent, lines up to about 120 characters, and comments in plain English. There's no formatter: match the file you're in.
- **Server or client.** Server Components unless a file starts with `"use client"`. Plain `lib/` modules have no directive. `lib/auth/actions.ts` starts with `"use server"`: everything it exports must be an async function (types are fine; constants are not). This plan adds one client component, `components/ui/FlashToast.tsx` (Task 9).
- **Next APIs.** Before using a Next API this plan names, read its page in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/` (AGENTS.md). This plan uses `cookies()` and `headers()` (`03-api-reference/04-functions/cookies.md`, `headers.md`), `connection()` (`connection.md`), `redirect()` and `RedirectType` (`redirect.md`), Server Actions in forms (`02-guides/server-actions.md`, `02-guides/forms.md`, `03-api-reference/01-directives/use-server.md`), reading the session behind `<Suspense>` (`02-guides/authentication-with-cache-components.md`) and a GET route handler (`03-api-reference/03-file-conventions/route.md`, `01-getting-started/15-route-handlers.md`). The first task that uses one says so in its first step.
- **Breakpoints.**
  - `wide:` is tablet and up; `desk:` is desktop. Never use `md:`, `lg:` or `sm:`.
  - In CSS, write `@media (min-width: 48rem) and (min-height: 30rem)` and `@media (min-width: 64rem) and (min-height: 30rem)`. Landscape phones are `@media (orientation: landscape) and (max-height: 29.99rem)`.
- **Page padding.** `.page` and the page's own class (`.signin-page`, `.account-page`) sit on one element, so the page class's padding *replaces* `.page`'s gutter. Write the whole content edge: 16px on phones, 40px from the tablet step.
- **Effects.** Never call `setState` directly in an effect body, and only touch `ref.current` in effects and handlers. The one effect in this plan (`FlashToast`) calls the toast store and writes a cookie; it sets no React state.
- **Headings.** One H1 per page. Sections are `<section aria-labelledby="…">` with one H2, visible or `sr-only`. Ids are unique across pages (`signin-…`, `account-…`, `account-privacy-…`), because Next keeps earlier pages hidden in the DOM.
- **Request-time parts.** Anything that reads cookies, headers, `searchParams` or the clock sits inside `<Suspense>`. Clock reads come after `await connection()` (decision 26); `getViewer()` does this for you. No `Date.now()`, `new Date()`, `Math.random()` or `crypto.random*` while prerendering: new tokens, codes and ids are made only in Server Actions. There's no `try`/`catch` round JSX: the React Compiler lint rejects it. Choose between states with `if`/`else` or a `switch`.
- **Server Actions.** Read every field with `text(formData, name)` (a missing field or a file is `""`). Never take identity from a field: the account comes from `getViewer()`/`requireAccount()`. Pass `next` through `safeNextPath` before any redirect. Call `redirect()` outside any `try`.
- **Forms.** Plain `<form action={serverAction} noValidate>`: `noValidate` so the server's error messages show rather than the browser's bubbles; inputs keep `required` so it's announced. Every input has a `<label>`; hints and errors join `aria-describedby`; an invalid field has `aria-invalid="true"`. Errors use the shared `ErrorSummary` and `FieldError` (Task 7).
- **Async components in tests.** jsdom can't render an async component inside a tree. Render `await Thing(props)` directly, or stand the async part in with `vi.mock`.
- **Server code in tests.** Use `tests/helpers/next-request.ts` (Task 4) to stand in for `next/headers`, `next/navigation` and `next/server`; call `resetSampleStore()` and `resetRequest(request)` in `beforeEach`. Fake only the date: `vi.useFakeTimers({ toFake: ["Date"] })` then `vi.setSystemTime(…)`; the setup file restores real timers after each test.
- **JSX text.** The `react/no-unescaped-entities` rule is on: write `&apos;` for an apostrophe inside JSX text (a string in an attribute or a prop is fine). JSX drops the space at a line break next to a tag, so end a text line with `{" "}` when the next line starts with a tag.
- **Links.** Pages on this site use `Link` from `next/link`. A plain `<a href>` is for a `#` jump inside the page, a file download from a route handler, and other websites. Links to session pages from the shell (`/login`, `/account`) and to Saved use `prefetch={false}`.
- **Accessible names.** Name computation trims each element's own text, so put spaces outside `sr-only` spans. Icons from `components/icons.tsx` are `aria-hidden`.
- **Contrast.**
  - Sky never carries text, icons or thin borders on white. Links on white are sky-dark; on panel and pale they're navy.
  - Danger red (`--color-danger`) is 6.6:1 on white: fine for text, borders and the delete button.
  - Small text on the star texture is white (the menu).
- **Motion.** Animate only `transform`/`opacity`. Hover effects only under `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`. This plan adds no animation.
- **Tap targets.** At least 44 × 44px, with 8px between targets. Links inside a paragraph are exempt (WCAG 2.5.8).
- **Lint.** `@next/next/no-html-link-for-pages` doesn't fire in this repo, so no plain `<a>` needs an `eslint-disable` comment. Don't add one: lint reports unused ones.
- **Class names.** New classes start with `form-`, `signin-`, `account-`, `link-button`, `btn-danger` or `menu-signout`. Before adding one, check it isn't taken: `grep -n "\.<name>\b" app/globals.css`.
- **CSS placement.**
  - New CSS goes at the end of `app/globals.css`, inside `@layer components`, under a banner: `/* --- Accounts: <name> (accounts plan, Task N, spec x) --- */`.
  - Never put `!important` on a transition or animation property: the motion off-switches rely on it.
- **Hidden pages in tests.** In e2e tests, find things with `getByRole`/`getByLabel` and `.filter({ visible: true })`; a CSS locator also counts the kept copies.
- **TDD.** Write the test, watch it fail, write the code, watch it pass.
- **Handing over.** Implementers don't run `git add` or `git commit`. The last step of each task lists the files and the commit message. The controller then:
  1. runs `git status --short` on its own, and checks that only the listed files changed (and `.claude/launch.json`, which is never staged);
  2. runs `git add` with the listed files (for Task 4's removal, `git add data/fixtures/viewer.ts` stages the deletion);
  3. runs `git commit` with the given message, ending with a blank line and `Co-Authored-By: <model> <noreply@anthropic.com>`.
  - One git command per tool call.
- **Looking at it.** Check visually on a production build (`pnpm build`, then `pnpm start --port 3201`) at 390 × 844, 768 × 1024 and 1280 × 800, using a throwaway Playwright script in your scratchpad. Stop the server afterwards (it's yours, not an install or build).
- **Measuring JS.** `pnpm e2e tests/e2e/performance.spec.ts --project=phone-390` builds, starts its own server on 3200, and prints one line per page, such as `sign-in page: {"script":…,"scriptBrotli":…}`. Record `scriptBrotli`.

```ts
/** What a Hub staff member can do on top of being a student (accounts spec 2.9). */
export type StaffRole = "mentor" | "admin";

/** Where a student's shop stands (accounts spec 2.9, selling spec 7). */
export type SellerStatus = "pending" | "approved" | "paused";

export type SellerProfile = {
  status: SellerStatus;
  /** The storefront's slug, once the shop has one. A shop waiting for approval has none yet. */
  sellerSlug: string | null;
};

/** A signed-in student (accounts spec 2.9). Every account is a student; some also sell, mentor or run Vossie. */
export type Account = {
  id: string;
  /** Lower case. Never shown to other students (messaging spec 2.11). */
  email: string;
  firstName: string;
  campusSlug: string;
  roles: readonly StaffRole[];
  seller: SellerProfile | null;
  /** ISO 8601 in UTC. */
  createdAt: string;
};

/** A record that the student agreed to the privacy policy (accounts spec 2.7). */
export type Consent = {
  /** The policy's version, e.g. "2026-10-draft". */
  version: string;
  /** ISO 8601 in UTC. */
  agreedAt: string;
};
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the store's row types**

Create `lib/data/rows.ts`:

```ts
import type { SellerStatus, StaffRole } from "@/lib/types";

// The sample store's rows (accounts spec section 4), shaped like the Supabase tables that will replace them: ids are
// text, times are ISO 8601 strings in UTC, and rows point at each other by id. Only lib/data reads or writes them.
// Later stages add their rows here (the accounts plan's "Store design").

export type AccountRow = {
  id: string;
  /** Lower case. */
  email: string;
  firstName: string;
  campusSlug: string;
  roles: StaffRole[];
  createdAt: string;
};

/** One per account that has applied to sell, keyed by the account's id. Stage F adds the shop's details. */
export type SellerProfileRow = {
  accountId: string;
  status: SellerStatus;
  sellerSlug: string | null;
  appliedAt: string;
};

export type ConsentRow = {
  id: string;
  accountId: string;
  version: string;
  agreedAt: string;
};

/** A signed-in browser. The key is the SHA-256 of the cookie's random token, so the store never holds the token. */
export type SessionRow = {
  key: string;
  accountId: string;
  createdAt: string;
  expiresAt: string;
};

export type SignInStep = "email" | "code" | "profile";

/** What went wrong on the last post (accounts spec 3.1), in the order the error summary lists them. */
export type SignInError =
  | "email-missing"
  | "email-domain"
  | "email-rate"
  | "consent-missing"
  | "code-missing"
  | "code-wrong"
  | "code-expired"
  | "code-rate"
  | "locked"
  | "first-name-missing"
  | "first-name-length"
  | "campus-missing";

/** A sign-in in progress, keyed by the SHA-256 of the `vossie_signin` cookie's token (accounts spec 2.2). */
export type SignInAttemptRow = {
  key: string;
  step: SignInStep;
  /** Lower case. On step 1 after an error, what was typed, so the field can show it again. */
  email: string;
  errors: SignInError[];
  /** True straight after "Send a new code", so step 2 can say so. */
  resent: boolean;
  /** The SHA-256 of the attempt's key and the code (lib/auth/secrets.ts). Never the code itself (spec 5). */
  codeHash: string | null;
  codeExpiresAt: string | null;
  /** Wrong tries on the current code. */
  wrongTries: number;
  /** When the privacy policy box was ticked, or null when it wasn't. */
  consentedAt: string | null;
  /** Step 3's answers after an error, so the fields can show them again. */
  firstName: string;
  campusSlug: string;
  expiresAt: string;
};

/**
 * Sample mode's stand-in for the email service (accounts spec 2.3): the last code "sent" for each attempt, keyed by
 * the attempt's key, so the sign-in page can show it. The real email service replaces this table; nothing else reads it.
 */
export type SampleEmailRow = {
  attemptKey: string;
  to: string;
  code: string;
  sentAt: string;
};

/**
 * A signed-in form's state after a post (accounts plan decision 3): its errors and typed values, or a notice such as
 * "saved". Keyed by `${sessionKey}:${form}`, so it belongs to one browser's session and one form.
 */
export type FormStateRow = {
  key: string;
  sessionKey: string;
  form: string;
  errors: string[];
  values: Record<string, string>;
  notice: string | null;
  expiresAt: string;
};
```

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { sampleAccounts, sampleConsents } from "./accounts";
import { campuses } from "./campuses";
import { sellers } from "./sellers";

const campusSlugs = new Set(campuses.map((campus) => campus.slug));
const sellersBySlug = new Map(sellers.map((seller) => [seller.slug, seller]));

describe("sample accounts (accounts spec 2.8)", () => {
  it("has the five sample accounts, all at eduvos.com, with unique ids", () => {
    expect(sampleAccounts.map(({ account }) => account.email)).toEqual([
      "student@eduvos.com",
      "seller@eduvos.com",
      "pending@eduvos.com",
      "mentor@eduvos.com",
      "admin@eduvos.com",
    ]);
    const ids = sampleAccounts.map(({ account }) => account.id);
    expect(new Set(ids).size).toBe(ids.length);
  });

  it("gives each the role the spec names", () => {
    const byEmail = new Map(sampleAccounts.map((sample) => [sample.account.email, sample]));
    expect(byEmail.get("student@eduvos.com")?.seller).toBeNull();
    expect(byEmail.get("student@eduvos.com")?.account.roles).toEqual([]);
    expect(byEmail.get("seller@eduvos.com")?.seller).toMatchObject({ status: "approved", sellerSlug: "tech-corner" });
    expect(byEmail.get("pending@eduvos.com")?.seller).toMatchObject({ status: "pending", sellerSlug: null });
    expect(byEmail.get("mentor@eduvos.com")?.account.roles).toEqual(["mentor"]);
    expect(byEmail.get("admin@eduvos.com")?.account.roles).toEqual(["admin"]);
  });

  it("puts every account on a real campus, and a shop on its owner's real storefront", () => {
    for (const { account, seller } of sampleAccounts) {
      expect(campusSlugs.has(account.campusSlug), account.email).toBe(true);
      expect(account.firstName.length, account.email).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);
      if (!seller) continue;
      expect(seller.accountId, account.email).toBe(account.id);
      if (seller.sellerSlug) expect(sellersBySlug.get(seller.sellerSlug)?.campusSlug, account.email).toBe(account.campusSlug);
    }
  });

  it("labels each sign-in button differently", () => {
    const labels = sampleAccounts.map((sample) => sample.label);
    expect(new Set(labels).size).toBe(labels.length);
  });

  it("records each account's consent to the October 2026 draft of the privacy policy", () => {
    expect(sampleConsents.map((consent) => consent.accountId)).toEqual(sampleAccounts.map(({ account }) => account.id));
    for (const consent of sampleConsents) expect(consent.version).toBe("2026-10-draft");
  });
});
```

```ts
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { sampleAccounts } from "@/data/fixtures/accounts";
import { resetSampleStore, sampleStore, STORE_VERSION } from "./store";

beforeEach(() => {
  resetSampleStore();
});

describe("the sample store (accounts spec 2.1)", () => {
  it("is seeded with the sample accounts, their consents and their shops, and nothing else", () => {
    const store = sampleStore();
    expect([...store.accounts.keys()]).toEqual(sampleAccounts.map(({ account }) => account.id));
    expect(store.consents.size).toBe(5);
    expect([...store.sellerProfiles.keys()]).toEqual(["acc-seller", "acc-pending"]);
    expect(store.sessions.size).toBe(0);
    expect(store.signInAttempts.size).toBe(0);
    expect(store.codeSends.size).toBe(0);
    expect(store.sampleEmails.size).toBe(0);
    expect(store.formStates.size).toBe(0);
  });

  it("is one store for the whole process, so a module loaded again still shares it", async () => {
    const first = sampleStore();
    vi.resetModules();
    const again = await import("./store");
    expect(again.sampleStore()).toBe(first);
  });

  it("copies the fixtures, so a write never changes them", () => {
    sampleStore().accounts.get("acc-student")!.firstName = "Changed";
    sampleStore().accounts.get("acc-mentor")!.roles.push("admin");
    expect(sampleAccounts[0].account.firstName).toBe("Thandi");
    expect(sampleAccounts[3].account.roles).toEqual(["mentor"]);
  });

  it("starts again from the fixtures when reset", () => {
    sampleStore().accounts.delete("acc-student");
    resetSampleStore();
    expect(sampleStore().accounts.has("acc-student")).toBe(true);
  });

  it("reseeds a store left behind by an older version, as next dev keeps globalThis across reloads", () => {
    const old = sampleStore();
    old.version = STORE_VERSION - 1;
    old.accounts.clear();
    expect(sampleStore().version).toBe(STORE_VERSION);
    expect(sampleStore().accounts.size).toBe(5);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Write the store**

Create `lib/data/store.ts`:

```ts
import { sampleAccounts, sampleConsents } from "@/data/fixtures/accounts";
import type {
  AccountRow,
  ConsentRow,
  FormStateRow,
  SampleEmailRow,
  SellerProfileRow,
  SessionRow,
  SignInAttemptRow,
} from "./rows";

/**
 * The sample store (accounts spec 2.1 and section 4), standing in for Supabase until there's a backend: one per server
 * process, seeded from the fixtures, gone when the server restarts. It lives on globalThis because each route, Server
 * Action and route handler may load this module separately, and they must all see the same data. Only lib/data reads
 * or writes it.
 *
 * Each table is a Map keyed by its primary key. To add one (stages E to G), follow the accounts plan's "Store design":
 * a row type in rows.ts, a field here, its seed in seedStore(), and a bump of STORE_VERSION.
 */
export type SampleStore = {
  version: number;
  /** By id. */
  accounts: Map<string, AccountRow>;
  /** By account id. */
  sellerProfiles: Map<string, SellerProfileRow>;
  /** By id. */
  consents: Map<string, ConsentRow>;
  /** By the SHA-256 of the session cookie's token. */
  sessions: Map<string, SessionRow>;
  /** By the SHA-256 of the sign-in cookie's token. */
  signInAttempts: Map<string, SignInAttemptRow>;
  /** Email → when codes were sent to it, for the limit of 5 an hour (spec 5). */
  codeSends: Map<string, string[]>;
  /** By attempt key: sample mode's outbox (spec 2.3). */
  sampleEmails: Map<string, SampleEmailRow>;
  /** By `${sessionKey}:${form}`. */
  formStates: Map<string, FormStateRow>;
};

/** Bump it when a table is added or a row changes shape, so `next dev` reseeds instead of keeping an old store. */
export const STORE_VERSION = 1;

type StoreHolder = { __vossieSampleStore?: SampleStore };

const holder = () => globalThis as unknown as StoreHolder;

/** The store, seeded on first use. */
export function sampleStore(): SampleStore {
  const current = holder().__vossieSampleStore;
  if (current?.version === STORE_VERSION) return current;
  const fresh = seedStore();
  holder().__vossieSampleStore = fresh;
  return fresh;
}

/** A fresh store from the fixtures. Unit tests call it before each test; the app never does. */
export function resetSampleStore(): void {
  holder().__vossieSampleStore = seedStore();
}

function seedStore(): SampleStore {
  // Copies, so a write to the store can never change the fixtures.
  const accounts = structuredClone(sampleAccounts);
  const consents = structuredClone(sampleConsents);
  return {
    version: STORE_VERSION,
    accounts: new Map(accounts.map(({ account }) => [account.id, account])),
    sellerProfiles: new Map(accounts.flatMap(({ seller }) => (seller ? [[seller.accountId, seller] as const] : []))),
    consents: new Map(consents.map((consent) => [consent.id, consent])),
    sessions: new Map(),
    signInAttempts: new Map(),
    codeSends: new Map(),
    sampleEmails: new Map(),
    formStates: new Map(),
  };
}
```

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { sampleConsents } from "@/data/fixtures/accounts";
import { emailDomains, isSampleMode, policyLabel, POLICY_VERSION } from "./config";

describe("sign-in settings (accounts spec 2.1, 2.4 and 2.7)", () => {
  it("is in sample mode unless VOSSIE_SAMPLE_MODE is 0", () => {
    expect(isSampleMode({})).toBe(true);
    expect(isSampleMode({ VOSSIE_SAMPLE_MODE: "1" })).toBe(true);
    expect(isSampleMode({ VOSSIE_SAMPLE_MODE: "0" })).toBe(false);
  });

  it("lets eduvos.com sign in unless VOSSIE_EMAIL_DOMAINS lists others", () => {
    expect(emailDomains({})).toEqual(["eduvos.com"]);
    expect(emailDomains({ VOSSIE_EMAIL_DOMAINS: " Student.Eduvos.com, @eduvos.ac.za ,," })).toEqual([
      "student.eduvos.com",
      "eduvos.ac.za",
    ]);
    expect(emailDomains({ VOSSIE_EMAIL_DOMAINS: " , " })).toEqual(["eduvos.com"]);
  });

  it("names the policy version a student agrees to, which the sample accounts' consents use", () => {
    expect(POLICY_VERSION).toBe("2026-10-draft");
    expect(policyLabel(POLICY_VERSION)).toBe("draft of October 2026");
    expect(policyLabel("2027-01")).toBe("2027-01");
    for (const consent of sampleConsents) expect(consent.version).toBe(POLICY_VERSION);
  });
});
```

```ts
import { afterEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { checkEmail, maskEmail, normaliseEmail } from "./email";

afterEach(() => {
  vi.unstubAllEnvs();
});

describe("Eduvos emails (accounts spec 2.4)", () => {
  it("tidies an email the way the store keeps it", () => {
    expect(normaliseEmail("  Thandi.M@EDUVOS.com ")).toBe("thandi.m@eduvos.com");
  });

  it("says when there's no email", () => {
    expect(checkEmail("", ["eduvos.com"])).toBe("missing");
  });

  it("accepts an address at a listed domain or a subdomain of one", () => {
    expect(checkEmail("thandi@eduvos.com", ["eduvos.com"])).toBe("ok");
    expect(checkEmail("thandi@student.eduvos.com", ["eduvos.com"])).toBe("ok");
    expect(checkEmail("thandi@eduvos.ac.za", ["eduvos.com", "eduvos.ac.za"])).toBe("ok");
  });

  it("refuses other domains, look-alikes and things that aren't addresses", () => {
    for (const email of [
      "thandi@gmail.com",
      "thandi@noteduvos.com",
      "thandi@eduvos.com.evil.example",
      "thandi@eduvos",
      "thandi",
      "@eduvos.com",
      "thandi@@eduvos.com",
      "th andi@eduvos.com",
      `${"a".repeat(250)}@eduvos.com`,
    ]) {
      expect(checkEmail(email, ["eduvos.com"]), email).toBe("domain");
    }
  });

  it("reads VOSSIE_EMAIL_DOMAINS when no list is given", () => {
    vi.stubEnv("VOSSIE_EMAIL_DOMAINS", "eduvos.ac.za");
    expect(checkEmail("thandi@eduvos.ac.za")).toBe("ok");
    expect(checkEmail("thandi@eduvos.com")).toBe("domain");
  });

  it("masks an email for \"We sent a code to …\" (spec 3.1)", () => {
    expect(maskEmail("thandi@eduvos.com")).toBe("t•••@eduvos.com");
    expect(maskEmail("t@student.eduvos.com")).toBe("t•••@student.eduvos.com");
  });
});
```

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { checkCampus, checkFirstName } from "./profile";

describe("first names (accounts spec 3.1)", () => {
  it("tidies the spaces in a first name", () => {
    expect(checkFirstName("  Thandi \n  Mary ")).toEqual({ value: "Thandi Mary", error: null });
  });

  it("asks for a name when there's none", () => {
    expect(checkFirstName("   ").error).toBe("first-name-missing");
  });

  it("takes 2 to 40 characters, counting each letter once", () => {
    expect(checkFirstName("T").error).toBe("first-name-length");
    expect(checkFirstName("Th").error).toBeNull();
    expect(checkFirstName("a".repeat(40)).error).toBeNull();
    expect(checkFirstName("a".repeat(41)).error).toBe("first-name-length");
    expect(checkFirstName("Zoë").error).toBeNull();
  });

  it("drops control characters", () => {
    expect(checkFirstName("Tha\u0000ndi")).toEqual({ value: "Thandi", error: null });
  });
});

describe("campuses (accounts spec 3.1)", () => {
  it("accepts only a campus on the list", () => {
    expect(checkCampus("midrand", ["midrand", "pretoria"])).toBe("midrand");
    expect(checkCampus("all", ["midrand", "pretoria"])).toBeNull();
    expect(checkCampus("", ["midrand", "pretoria"])).toBeNull();
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the tokens and codes' test**

Create `lib/auth/secrets.test.ts`:

```ts
import { randomInt, timingSafeEqual } from "node:crypto";
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { codeMatches, hashCode, hashToken, newCode, newToken } from "./secrets";

vi.mock("node:crypto", async (importOriginal) => {
  const actual = await importOriginal<typeof import("node:crypto")>();
  return { ...actual, randomInt: vi.fn(actual.randomInt), timingSafeEqual: vi.fn(actual.timingSafeEqual) };
});

describe("tokens and codes (accounts spec 4 and 5)", () => {
  it("makes random UUID tokens", () => {
    const tokens = new Set(Array.from({ length: 20 }, () => newToken()));
    expect(tokens.size).toBe(20);
    for (const token of tokens) expect(token).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f-]{36}$/);
  });

  it("keeps a token as its SHA-256, in hex", () => {
    expect(hashToken("abc")).toBe("ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad");
  });

  it("makes 6-digit codes from crypto.randomInt, keeping leading zeros", () => {
    for (let n = 0; n < 50; n++) expect(newCode()).toMatch(/^\d{6}$/);
    vi.mocked(randomInt).mockReturnValueOnce(42 as never);
    expect(newCode()).toBe("000042");
  });

  it("hashes a code with its attempt's key, so the same code hashes differently for each attempt", () => {
    expect(hashCode("key-a", "123456")).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/);
    expect(hashCode("key-a", "123456")).not.toBe(hashCode("key-b", "123456"));
  });

  it("checks a code in constant time", () => {
    const stored = hashCode("key-a", "042137");
    expect(codeMatches("key-a", "042137", stored)).toBe(true);
    expect(codeMatches("key-a", "042138", stored)).toBe(false);
    expect(codeMatches("key-b", "042137", stored)).toBe(false);
    expect(vi.mocked(timingSafeEqual)).toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it("never matches a broken stored hash", () => {
    expect(codeMatches("key-a", "042137", "not-hex")).toBe(false);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the messages' test**

Create `lib/auth/messages.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { errorFor, formErrors, SIGN_IN_FIELDS } from "./messages";

describe("error messages (accounts spec 3.1, plan decision 7)", () => {
  it("words each sign-in error as the spec does, linked to its field", () => {
    expect(formErrors(["email-missing", "consent-missing"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS)).toEqual([
      { message: "Enter your email address.", fieldId: "signin-email" },
      { message: "Tick the box to agree to the privacy policy.", fieldId: "signin-consent" },
    ]);
    expect(formErrors(["email-domain"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS)).toEqual([
      { message: "Use your Eduvos email address.", fieldId: "signin-email" },
    ]);
    expect(formErrors(["code-missing", "code-expired"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS)).toEqual([
      { message: "Enter the 6-digit code.", fieldId: "signin-code" },
      { message: "That code has expired. Send a new one.", fieldId: "signin-code" },
    ]);
    expect(formErrors(["locked"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS)).toEqual([
      { message: "Too many tries. Start again.", fieldId: "signin-email" },
    ]);
  });

  it("counts the tries left on a wrong code", () => {
    expect(formErrors(["code-wrong"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS, 1)[0].message).toBe("That code isn't right. 4 tries left.");
    expect(formErrors(["code-wrong"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS, 4)[0].message).toBe("That code isn't right. 1 try left.");
  });

  it("says when the hour's codes are used up, on whichever step asked", () => {
    const rate = "You've asked for 5 codes in the last hour. Try again later.";
    expect(formErrors(["email-rate"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS)).toEqual([{ message: rate, fieldId: "signin-email" }]);
    expect(formErrors(["code-rate"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS)).toEqual([{ message: rate, fieldId: "signin-code" }]);
  });

  it("words step 3's and the account forms' errors", () => {
    const fields = { firstName: "details-first-name", campus: "details-campus", confirm: "delete-confirm" };
    expect(formErrors(["first-name-missing", "first-name-length", "campus-missing", "confirm-missing"], fields)).toEqual([
      { message: "Enter your first name.", fieldId: "details-first-name" },
      { message: "Your first name must be 2 to 40 characters.", fieldId: "details-first-name" },
      { message: "Choose your campus.", fieldId: "details-campus" },
      { message: "Tick the box to confirm you understand.", fieldId: "delete-confirm" },
    ]);
  });

  it("leaves an error unlinked when the form has no such field, and drops codes it doesn't know", () => {
    expect(formErrors(["consent-missing"], { email: "x" })).toEqual([
      { message: "Tick the box to agree to the privacy policy.", fieldId: null },
    ]);
    expect(formErrors(["nonsense", "toString"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS)).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("finds the message for one field, for the line under it", () => {
    const errors = formErrors(["email-domain", "consent-missing"], SIGN_IN_FIELDS);
    expect(errorFor(errors, "signin-consent")).toBe("Tick the box to agree to the privacy policy.");
    expect(errorFor(errors, "signin-code")).toBeUndefined();
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Write the settings**

Create `lib/auth/config.ts`:

```ts
// Sign-in settings (accounts spec 2.1 to 2.7 and section 5). Plain values and functions: safe anywhere on the server.

/** The privacy policy version a student agrees to at sign-in (spec 2.7). Change it when the policy changes. */
export const POLICY_VERSION = "2026-10-draft";

const POLICY_LABELS: ReadonlyMap<string, string> = new Map([["2026-10-draft", "draft of October 2026"]]);

/** How the privacy settings page names a policy version: "draft of October 2026" (spec 3.3). */
export function policyLabel(version: string): string {
  return POLICY_LABELS.get(version) ?? version;
}

/** A code lasts 10 minutes, and 5 wrong tries end the attempt (spec 2.2). */
export const CODE_TTL_MS = 10 * 60 * 1000;
export const MAX_WRONG_TRIES = 5;
/** At most 5 codes per email per hour, in sample mode (spec 5). */
export const CODES_PER_HOUR = 5;
/** A sign-in attempt, all three steps, must finish within 30 minutes of starting. */
export const ATTEMPT_TTL_MS = 30 * 60 * 1000;
/** A session lasts 30 days (spec 2.5). */
export const SESSION_TTL_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
/** A signed-in form's errors or notice last 10 minutes (plan decision 3). */
export const FORM_STATE_TTL_MS = 10 * 60 * 1000;
/** A flash message's cookie lasts a minute (plan decision 12). */
export const FLASH_TTL_SECONDS = 60;

type Env = Readonly<Record<string, string | undefined>>;

/** Sample mode (spec 2.1): on unless VOSSIE_SAMPLE_MODE is "0". */
export function isSampleMode(env: Env = process.env): boolean {
  return env.VOSSIE_SAMPLE_MODE !== "0";
}

/** The domains that can sign in (spec 2.4): VOSSIE_EMAIL_DOMAINS, comma-separated, or else eduvos.com. */
export function emailDomains(env: Env = process.env): string[] {
  const listed = (env.VOSSIE_EMAIL_DOMAINS ?? "")
    .split(",")
    .map((domain) => domain.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/^@/, ""))
    .filter(Boolean);
  return listed.length > 0 ? listed : ["eduvos.com"];
}
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Write the email rules**

Create `lib/auth/email.ts`:

```ts
import { emailDomains } from "./config";

const MAX_EMAIL_LENGTH = 254;
const EMAIL_SHAPE = /^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$/;

/** An email as the store keeps it: trimmed and lower case. */
export function normaliseEmail(raw: string): string {
  return raw.trim().toLowerCase();
}

export type EmailCheck = "ok" | "missing" | "domain";

/**
 * Whether a normalised email can sign in (spec 2.4): an address at one of the domains, or at a subdomain of one.
 * Anything that isn't an address gets the same answer as a wrong domain: "Use your Eduvos email address."
 */
export function checkEmail(email: string, domains: readonly string[] = emailDomains()): EmailCheck {
  if (email === "") return "missing";
  if (email.length > MAX_EMAIL_LENGTH || !EMAIL_SHAPE.test(email)) return "domain";
  const domain = email.slice(email.lastIndexOf("@") + 1);
  return domains.some((allowed) => domain === allowed || domain.endsWith(`.${allowed}`)) ? "ok" : "domain";
}

/** "thandi@eduvos.com" → "t•••@eduvos.com", for "We sent a code to …" (spec 3.1). */
export function maskEmail(email: string): string {
  return `${email.slice(0, 1)}•••${email.slice(email.lastIndexOf("@"))}`;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 10: Write the name and campus rules**

Create `lib/auth/profile.ts`:

```ts
import type { SignInError } from "@/lib/data/rows";

export type FirstNameCheck = {
  /** The name with its spaces tidied and control characters dropped. */
  value: string;
  error: Extract<SignInError, "first-name-missing" | "first-name-length"> | null;
};

/** A first name (spec 3.1): 2 to 40 characters, once the spaces are tidied. Each letter counts once, accents too. */
export function checkFirstName(raw: string): FirstNameCheck {
  const value = raw.replace(/\s+/g, " ").replace(/\p{Cc}/gu, "").trim();
  if (value === "") return { value, error: "first-name-missing" };
  const length = [...value].length;
  return { value, error: length < 2 || length > 40 ? "first-name-length" : null };
}

/** The campus slug, when it's one of the campuses (spec 3.1: a select of the 13). Otherwise null. */
export function checkCampus(raw: string, campusSlugs: readonly string[]): string | null {
  return campusSlugs.includes(raw) ? raw : null;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 11: Write the tokens and codes**

Create `lib/auth/secrets.ts`:

```ts
import { createHash, randomInt, randomUUID, timingSafeEqual } from "node:crypto";

// Tokens and codes (accounts spec section 4 and 5). Making one reads randomness, so only Server Actions call
// newToken() and newCode(), never a render. Hashing is deterministic and safe anywhere on the server.

/** A random token for a cookie: a session's or a sign-in attempt's. */
export function newToken(): string {
  return randomUUID();
}

/** What the store keeps instead of a token: its SHA-256, in hex (plan decision 4). */
export function hashToken(token: string): string {
  return createHash("sha256").update(token).digest("hex");
}

/** A 6-digit code, leading zeros kept. */
export function newCode(): string {
  return randomInt(0, 1_000_000).toString().padStart(6, "0");
}

/** A code's SHA-256, salted with its attempt's key, so the same code hashes differently for every attempt (spec 5). */
export function hashCode(attemptKey: string, code: string): string {
  return createHash("sha256").update(`${attemptKey}:${code}`).digest("hex");
}

/** Whether `code` is the attempt's code. The hashes are compared in constant time (spec 5). */
export function codeMatches(attemptKey: string, code: string, storedHash: string): boolean {
  const expected = Buffer.from(storedHash, "hex");
  const actual = Buffer.from(hashCode(attemptKey, code), "hex");
  return expected.length === actual.length && timingSafeEqual(expected, actual);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 12: Write the messages**

Create `lib/auth/messages.ts`:

```ts
import { MAX_WRONG_TRIES } from "./config";

/** One line in an error summary, and the id of the field it belongs to (null when the form has no such field). */
export type FormError = { message: string; fieldId: string | null };

/** The sign-in page's field ids (components/auth/SignInSteps.tsx), so each error can link to its field. */
export const SIGN_IN_FIELDS = {
  email: "signin-email",
  consent: "signin-consent",
  code: "signin-code",
  firstName: "signin-first-name",
  campus: "signin-campus",
} as const;

const RATE = "You've asked for 5 codes in the last hour. Try again later.";

// The words for each error code (spec 3.1; plan decision 7 for the ones the spec doesn't give) and the field each
// belongs to. "code-wrong" is worded in wrongCode(), because it counts the tries left. A Map, so a stray code such as
// "toString" finds nothing.
const MESSAGES: ReadonlyMap<string, { message: string; field: string }> = new Map([
  ["email-missing", { message: "Enter your email address.", field: "email" }],
  ["email-domain", { message: "Use your Eduvos email address.", field: "email" }],
  ["email-rate", { message: RATE, field: "email" }],
  ["consent-missing", { message: "Tick the box to agree to the privacy policy.", field: "consent" }],
  ["code-missing", { message: "Enter the 6-digit code.", field: "code" }],
  ["code-expired", { message: "That code has expired. Send a new one.", field: "code" }],
  ["code-rate", { message: RATE, field: "code" }],
  ["locked", { message: "Too many tries. Start again.", field: "email" }],
  ["first-name-missing", { message: "Enter your first name.", field: "firstName" }],
  ["first-name-length", { message: "Your first name must be 2 to 40 characters.", field: "firstName" }],
  ["campus-missing", { message: "Choose your campus.", field: "campus" }],
  ["confirm-missing", { message: "Tick the box to confirm you understand.", field: "confirm" }],
]);

function wrongCode(wrongTries: number): string {
  const left = MAX_WRONG_TRIES - wrongTries;
  return `That code isn't right. ${left} ${left === 1 ? "try" : "tries"} left.`;
}

/**
 * The words for a form's error codes, in order, each linked to its field's id when the form has that field.
 * `wrongTries` is the tries used on the current code, for "That code isn't right. 4 tries left."
 */
export function formErrors(
  codes: readonly string[],
  fieldIds: Readonly<Record<string, string>>,
  wrongTries = 0,
): FormError[] {
  return codes.flatMap((code) => {
    const known = code === "code-wrong" ? { message: wrongCode(wrongTries), field: "code" } : MESSAGES.get(code);
    if (!known) return [];
    return [{ message: known.message, fieldId: Object.hasOwn(fieldIds, known.field) ? fieldIds[known.field] : null }];
  });
}

/** The message that sits under one field, if it has one. */
export function errorFor(errors: readonly FormError[], fieldId: string): string | undefined {
  return errors.find((error) => error.fieldId === fieldId)?.message;
}
```

```ts

// en-GB writes "October 2026".
const SAST_MONTH_YEAR = new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-GB", {
  timeZone: "Africa/Johannesburg",
  month: "long",
  year: "numeric",
});

/** A stored UTC time as its month in South Africa: "October 2026" (accounts spec 3.2). It reads no clock. */
export function sastMonthYear(iso: string): string {
  return SAST_MONTH_YEAR.format(new Date(iso));
}

const SAST_CLOCK = new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-GB", {
  timeZone: "Africa/Johannesburg",
  hour: "2-digit",
  minute: "2-digit",
  hourCycle: "h23",
});

/** A stored UTC time as the time of day in South Africa, 24-hour: "10:42" (accounts spec 3.3). It reads no clock. */
export function sastClock(iso: string): string {
  return SAST_CLOCK.format(new Date(iso));
}
```

```ts
/** A sign-in attempt for tests: on step 1, with no errors, and running out in 2099. Override what the test is about. */
export function makeSignInAttempt(overrides: Partial<SignInAttemptRow> = {}): SignInAttemptRow {
  return {
    key: "attempt-key",
    step: "email",
    email: "",
    errors: [],
    resent: false,
    codeHash: null,
    codeExpiresAt: null,
    wrongTries: 0,
    consentedAt: null,
    firstName: "",
    campusSlug: "",
    expiresAt: "2099-01-01T00:00:00.000Z",
    ...overrides,
  };
}
```

```ts
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { makeSignInAttempt } from "@/tests/helpers/fakes";
import {
  createAccount,
  deleteAccountData,
  exportAccountData,
  findAccountByEmail,
  getAccount,
  getAccountActivity,
  getLatestConsent,
  getSampleSignIns,
  recordConsent,
  restoreSampleAccount,
  updateAccountDetails,
} from "./accounts";
import { saveFormState } from "./form-states";
import { createSession } from "./sessions";
import { recordCodeSent, saveSignInAttempt } from "./sign-in";
import { resetSampleStore, sampleStore } from "./store";

const NOW = new Date("2026-10-14T08:42:00.000Z");
const IN_30_DAYS = new Date("2026-11-13T08:42:00.000Z");

beforeEach(() => {
  resetSampleStore();
});

describe("accounts (accounts spec 2.6 and 2.9)", () => {
  it("finds an account by id or by email, with its shop and roles", async () => {
    expect(await findAccountByEmail("seller@eduvos.com")).toEqual({
      id: "acc-seller",
      email: "seller@eduvos.com",
      firstName: "Sipho",
      campusSlug: "midrand",
      roles: [],
      seller: { status: "approved", sellerSlug: "tech-corner" },
      createdAt: "2026-03-02T09:15:00.000Z",
    });
    expect((await getAccount("acc-mentor"))?.roles).toEqual(["mentor"]);
    expect((await getAccount("acc-pending"))?.seller).toEqual({ status: "pending", sellerSlug: null });
    expect(await findAccountByEmail("nobody@eduvos.com")).toBeNull();
    expect(await getAccount("acc-nope")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("hands out copies, so changing one never changes the store", async () => {
    const account = (await getAccount("acc-student"))!;
    (account.roles as string[]).push("admin");
    expect((await getAccount("acc-student"))!.roles).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("creates a student account with a random id, no roles and no shop", async () => {
    const account = await createAccount({ email: "new@eduvos.com", firstName: "Ayanda", campusSlug: "pretoria", now: NOW });
    expect(account).toMatchObject({
      email: "new@eduvos.com",
      firstName: "Ayanda",
      campusSlug: "pretoria",
      roles: [],
      seller: null,
      createdAt: NOW.toISOString(),
    });
    expect(account.id).toMatch(/^acc-[0-9a-f-]{36}$/);
    expect(await findAccountByEmail("new@eduvos.com")).toEqual(account);
  });

  it("changes the first name and campus", async () => {
    expect(await updateAccountDetails("acc-student", { firstName: "Thandiwe", campusSlug: "pretoria" })).toMatchObject({
      firstName: "Thandiwe",
      campusSlug: "pretoria",
    });
    expect(await getAccount("acc-student")).toMatchObject({ firstName: "Thandiwe", campusSlug: "pretoria" });
    expect(await updateAccountDetails("acc-nope", { firstName: "Zola", campusSlug: "pretoria" })).toBeNull();
  });

  it("has no activity for the context strip yet: zeros until messaging and selling (spec 2.9)", async () => {
    expect(await getAccountActivity("acc-student")).toEqual({ newMessages: 0, salesToConfirm: 0, savedItemsBack: 0 });
    expect(await getAccountActivity("acc-nope")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("lists the sample accounts' buttons (spec 2.8)", async () => {
    expect(await getSampleSignIns()).toEqual([
      { email: "student@eduvos.com", label: "Student, Midrand" },
      { email: "seller@eduvos.com", label: "Seller, Tech corner" },
      { email: "pending@eduvos.com", label: "Seller waiting for approval" },
      { email: "mentor@eduvos.com", label: "Mentor" },
      { email: "admin@eduvos.com", label: "Admin" },
    ]);
  });
});

describe("consent (accounts spec 2.7)", () => {
  it("records consent once per policy version, and shows the newest", async () => {
    const account = await createAccount({ email: "new@eduvos.com", firstName: "Ayanda", campusSlug: "pretoria", now: NOW });
    expect(await getLatestConsent(account.id)).toBeNull();
    await recordConsent(account.id, "2026-10-draft", NOW);
    await recordConsent(account.id, "2026-10-draft", new Date("2026-10-20T10:00:00.000Z"));
    expect(await getLatestConsent(account.id)).toEqual({ version: "2026-10-draft", agreedAt: NOW.toISOString() });
    await recordConsent(account.id, "2027-01", new Date("2027-01-05T09:00:00.000Z"));
    expect(await getLatestConsent(account.id)).toEqual({ version: "2027-01", agreedAt: "2027-01-05T09:00:00.000Z" });
  });

  it("has the sample accounts' seeded consent", async () => {
    expect(await getLatestConsent("acc-student")).toEqual({
      version: "2026-10-draft",
      agreedAt: "2026-10-14T08:00:00.000Z",
    });
  });
});

describe("the data export (accounts spec 3.3, plan decision 20)", () => {
  it("holds the account, its consent, and when its sessions started and end, with no ids", async () => {
    await createSession({ key: "session-key-1", accountId: "acc-seller", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    const data = await exportAccountData("acc-seller", NOW);
    expect(data).toEqual({
      exportedAt: NOW.toISOString(),
      account: {
        email: "seller@eduvos.com",
        firstName: "Sipho",
        campus: "midrand",
        roles: [],
        memberSince: "2026-03-02T09:15:00.000Z",
        shop: { status: "approved", storefront: "tech-corner" },
      },
      consents: [{ policy: "2026-10-draft", agreedAt: "2026-10-14T08:00:00.000Z" }],
      sessions: [{ signedInAt: NOW.toISOString(), expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS.toISOString() }],
    });
    const text = JSON.stringify(data);
    expect(text).not.toContain("session-key-1");
    expect(text).not.toContain("acc-seller");
  });

  it("has nothing for an account that doesn't exist", async () => {
    expect(await exportAccountData("acc-nope", NOW)).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe("deleting an account (accounts spec 2.13)", () => {
  it("removes the account and everything that hangs off it, and nothing of anyone else's", async () => {
    await createSession({ key: "mine", accountId: "acc-pending", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    await createSession({ key: "theirs", accountId: "acc-student", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    await saveFormState(
      { sessionKey: "mine", form: "details", errors: ["first-name-missing"], values: {}, notice: null, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS.toISOString() },
      NOW,
    );
    await saveSignInAttempt(makeSignInAttempt({ key: "attempt", email: "pending@eduvos.com", step: "code" }), NOW);
    await recordCodeSent("pending@eduvos.com", NOW);

    await deleteAccountData("acc-pending");

    const store = sampleStore();
    expect(store.accounts.has("acc-pending")).toBe(false);
    expect(store.sellerProfiles.has("acc-pending")).toBe(false);
    expect([...store.consents.values()].some((row) => row.accountId === "acc-pending")).toBe(false);
    expect([...store.sessions.keys()]).toEqual(["theirs"]);
    expect(store.formStates.size).toBe(0);
    expect(store.signInAttempts.has("attempt")).toBe(false);
    expect(store.codeSends.has("pending@eduvos.com")).toBe(false);
    expect(store.accounts.size).toBe(4);
    expect(store.consents.size).toBe(4);
  });
});

describe("sample accounts come back (plan decision 15)", () => {
  it("puts a deleted sample account back from the fixtures, with its shop and consent", async () => {
    await deleteAccountData("acc-seller");
    expect(await restoreSampleAccount("seller@eduvos.com")).toMatchObject({
      id: "acc-seller",
      firstName: "Sipho",
      seller: { status: "approved", sellerSlug: "tech-corner" },
    });
    expect(await getLatestConsent("acc-seller")).not.toBeNull();
  });

  it("leaves a sample account that's still there as it is", async () => {
    await updateAccountDetails("acc-student", { firstName: "Thandiwe", campusSlug: "midrand" });
    expect((await restoreSampleAccount("student@eduvos.com"))?.firstName).toBe("Thandiwe");
  });

  it("never makes a second account with a sample address", async () => {
    await deleteAccountData("acc-student");
    const own = await createAccount({ email: "student@eduvos.com", firstName: "Zola", campusSlug: "midrand", now: NOW });
    expect((await restoreSampleAccount("student@eduvos.com"))?.id).toBe(own.id);
    expect([...sampleStore().accounts.values()].filter((row) => row.email === "student@eduvos.com")).toHaveLength(1);
  });

  it("is only for the sample addresses", async () => {
    expect(await restoreSampleAccount("someone@eduvos.com")).toBeNull();
  });
});
```

```ts
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { saveFormState } from "./form-states";
import { createSession, deleteSession, findSessionAccountId, listSessions } from "./sessions";
import { resetSampleStore, sampleStore } from "./store";

const NOW = new Date("2026-10-14T08:42:00.000Z");
const IN_30_DAYS = new Date("2026-11-13T08:42:00.000Z");

beforeEach(() => {
  resetSampleStore();
});

describe("sessions (accounts spec 2.5)", () => {
  it("finds the account a session belongs to until it runs out", async () => {
    await createSession({ key: "key-1", accountId: "acc-student", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    expect(await findSessionAccountId("key-1", NOW)).toBe("acc-student");
    expect(await findSessionAccountId("key-1", new Date("2026-11-13T08:41:59.000Z"))).toBe("acc-student");
    expect(await findSessionAccountId("key-1", IN_30_DAYS)).toBeNull();
    expect(await findSessionAccountId("key-2", NOW)).toBeNull();
  });

  it("lists an account's sessions, oldest first, and no one else's", async () => {
    const later = new Date("2026-10-15T08:00:00.000Z");
    await createSession({ key: "later", accountId: "acc-student", createdAt: later, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    await createSession({ key: "earlier", accountId: "acc-student", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    await createSession({ key: "theirs", accountId: "acc-admin", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    expect((await listSessions("acc-student")).map((row) => row.key)).toEqual(["earlier", "later"]);
  });

  it("tidies away sessions that have run out when another starts", async () => {
    await createSession({
      key: "old",
      accountId: "acc-student",
      createdAt: new Date("2026-09-01T08:00:00.000Z"),
      expiresAt: new Date("2026-10-01T08:00:00.000Z"),
    });
    await createSession({ key: "new", accountId: "acc-student", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    expect([...sampleStore().sessions.keys()]).toEqual(["new"]);
  });

  it("signs one browser out, and its forms' state goes with it", async () => {
    await createSession({ key: "mine", accountId: "acc-student", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    await createSession({ key: "other", accountId: "acc-student", createdAt: NOW, expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS });
    const state = { form: "details", errors: [], values: {}, notice: "saved", expiresAt: IN_30_DAYS.toISOString() };
    await saveFormState({ sessionKey: "mine", ...state }, NOW);
    await saveFormState({ sessionKey: "other", ...state }, NOW);
    await deleteSession("mine");
    expect(await findSessionAccountId("mine", NOW)).toBeNull();
    expect(await findSessionAccountId("other", NOW)).toBe("acc-student");
    expect([...sampleStore().formStates.values()].map((row) => row.sessionKey)).toEqual(["other"]);
  });
});
```

```ts
import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
import { makeSignInAttempt } from "@/tests/helpers/fakes";
import {
  countCodesSent,
  deleteSignInAttempt,
  getSampleEmail,
  getSignInAttempt,
  recordCodeSent,
  saveSampleEmail,
  saveSignInAttempt,
} from "./sign-in";
import { resetSampleStore, sampleStore } from "./store";

const NOW = new Date("2026-10-14T08:42:00.000Z");

beforeEach(() => {
  resetSampleStore();
});

describe("sign-in attempts (accounts spec 2.2)", () => {
  it("saves and finds an attempt, handing out a copy", async () => {
    await saveSignInAttempt(makeSignInAttempt({ key: "a1", email: "thandi@eduvos.com" }), NOW);
    const found = await getSignInAttempt("a1", NOW);
    expect(found?.email).toBe("thandi@eduvos.com");
    found!.errors.push("locked");
    expect((await getSignInAttempt("a1", NOW))?.errors).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("forgets an attempt once it has run out, and tidies it away with its sample email on the next save", async () => {
    const early = new Date("2026-10-14T08:00:00.000Z");
    await saveSignInAttempt(makeSignInAttempt({ key: "old", expiresAt: "2026-10-14T08:30:00.000Z" }), early);
    await saveSampleEmail({ attemptKey: "old", to: "t@eduvos.com", code: "123456", sentAt: early.toISOString() });
    expect(await getSignInAttempt("old", new Date("2026-10-14T08:29:59.000Z"))).not.toBeNull();
    expect(await getSignInAttempt("old", new Date("2026-10-14T08:30:00.000Z"))).toBeNull();
    await saveSignInAttempt(makeSignInAttempt({ key: "new" }), NOW);
    expect(sampleStore().signInAttempts.has("old")).toBe(false);
    expect(await getSampleEmail("old")).toBeNull();
  });

  it("ends an attempt, and its sample email goes with it", async () => {
    await saveSignInAttempt(makeSignInAttempt({ key: "a1" }), NOW);
    await saveSampleEmail({ attemptKey: "a1", to: "t@eduvos.com", code: "123456", sentAt: NOW.toISOString() });
    await deleteSignInAttempt("a1");
    expect(await getSignInAttempt("a1", NOW)).toBeNull();
    expect(await getSampleEmail("a1")).toBeNull();
  });
});

describe("the code count (accounts spec 5)", () => {
  it("counts the codes sent to an email in the last hour", async () => {
    await recordCodeSent("t@eduvos.com", new Date("2026-10-14T07:40:00.000Z"));
    await recordCodeSent("t@eduvos.com", new Date("2026-10-14T08:00:00.000Z"));
    await recordCodeSent("t@eduvos.com", new Date("2026-10-14T08:30:00.000Z"));
    await recordCodeSent("other@eduvos.com", NOW);
    // 07:40 is more than an hour before 08:42.
    expect(await countCodesSent("t@eduvos.com", NOW)).toBe(2);
    expect(await countCodesSent("nobody@eduvos.com", NOW)).toBe(0);
  });

  it("forgets sends older than an hour as it records a new one", async () => {
    await recordCodeSent("t@eduvos.com", new Date("2026-10-14T06:00:00.000Z"));
    await recordCodeSent("t@eduvos.com", NOW);
    expect(sampleStore().codeSends.get("t@eduvos.com")).toEqual([NOW.toISOString()]);
  });
});

describe("sample mode's outbox (accounts spec 2.3, plan decision 5)", () => {
  it("keeps the last code sent for each attempt", async () => {
    await saveSampleEmail({ attemptKey: "a1", to: "t@eduvos.com", code: "111111", sentAt: NOW.toISOString() });
    await saveSampleEmail({ attemptKey: "a1", to: "t@eduvos.com", code: "222222", sentAt: NOW.toISOString() });
    expect((await getSampleEmail("a1"))?.code).toBe("222222");
    expect(await getSampleEmail("a2")).toBeNull();
  });
});
```
